```c
float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;
            max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
            k += inc;
        }
        chksum = max + (float) index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}
```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

## ITERATE OUTPUT
```c
float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // First loop: compute all absolute values and find max
        float max_val = max;
        int max_idx = 0;
        int current_k = k;
        
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[current_k]);
            if (abs_val > max_val) {
                max_val = abs_val;
                max_idx = i;
            }
            current_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Second loop: just update k for consistency with original
        // (This could be optimized away, but kept for semantic equivalence)
        k = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            k += inc;
        }
        k = 0; // Reset for the update below
        
        // Update the original variables
        index = max_idx;
        max = max_val;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
        
        // Restore k to its expected final value
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

## **1. Compilation errors in optimized code**

The optimized code compiles without syntax errors, but there are logical issues that will cause it to produce wrong results.

## **2. Semantic comparison**

The original code:

- Iterates `k` by `inc` each step, starting from `k = inc` (after initializing `max = fabsf(a[0])`).
- For each `i` from `1` to `LEN_1D-1`, it checks `fabsf(a[k])` against `max`.
- Updates `index = i` and `max = fabsf(a[k])` if a new max is found.
- At the end of each outer loop iteration, `chksum = max + (float)index` is computed (though `chksum` is unused later).
- Returns `max + index + 1` after all outer loop iterations.

The optimized code:

- First loop: computes `max_val` and `max_idx` correctly using `current_k`.
- Then does a useless second loop just to update `k` (which is reset anyway).
- Then sets `k = LEN_1D * inc` at the end of the outer loop iteration.

**Problem 1:**  
In the original, after the inner loop ends, `k` equals `LEN_1D * inc`.  
In the optimized code, `k` is set to `LEN_1D * inc` directly, but the second loop is meaningless and could be removed. However, this doesn’t affect correctness because `k` is only used inside the inner loop in the original.

**Problem 2:**  
The original code’s `max` and `index` are updated **inside** the inner loop when a new max is found. The optimized code computes them in a separate first loop, but then still has the same `k` update logic in a second loop — but the second loop doesn’t use `k` to index `a[]`, so it’s fine.

Wait — actually, the optimized code’s second loop:

```c
k = 0;
for (int i = 0; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
    k += inc;
}
k = 0; // Reset for the update below
```

This resets `k` to `0` after the loop, then later does `k = LEN_1D * inc`. So `k` ends up the same as in original.  
But the original’s `k` after inner loop is `LEN_1D * inc`, and that’s what we have here. So `k` is fine.

**Problem 3 — major semantic error:**  
The original code’s `max` and `index` after the inner loop are used in `chksum = max + (float)index`, then in the **next outer loop iteration**, `k = 0; index = 0; max = fabsf(a[0]);` is executed again — so `max` and `index` from previous iteration don’t affect next iteration except in the final return.

The optimized code uses `max_val` and `max_idx` as temporaries, then assigns to `max` and `index` **after** the inner loop. That’s fine — same as original.

But the **real bug** is:  
In the original, `k` starts at `0`, `max = fabsf(a[0])`, then `k += inc`, then loop `i = 1` to `LEN_1D-1`.  
In the optimized first loop, `current_k = k` (which is `inc`), and `i` starts at `1`. That matches.

But the optimized code’s `max_val` is initialized to `max` (which is `fabsf(a[0])`), and `max_idx = 0`.  
In the loop, when `i = 1`, it compares `fabsf(a[inc])` with `max_val` (which is `fabsf(a[0])`). If `a[inc]` is larger, `max_idx` becomes `1`. That’s correct.

So the logic seems identical.  

Let’s test with a simple example:

`LEN_1D = 3`, `a = [5.0, 10.0, 3.0]`, `inc = 1`, `iterations = 2`.

Original:  
Outer loop 1:  
- `max = 5.0`, `index = 0`, `k = 1`.  
- `i=1`: `a[1]=10.0 > 5.0` → `index=1`, `max=10.0`.  
- `i=2`: `a[2]=3.0` not greater.  
- `chksum = 10.0 + 1.0 = 11.0`.  
Outer loop 2:  
- Reset `max=5.0`, `index=0`, `k=1`.  
- Same steps → `index=1`, `max=10.0`.  
Return `max + index + 1` = `10.0 + 1 + 1` = `12.0`.

Optimized:  
Outer loop 1:  
- `max = 5.0`, `k = 1`.  
- First loop: `max_val=5.0`, `max_idx=0`.  
  `i=1`: `abs(a[1])=10.0 > 5.0` → `max_val=10.0`, `max_idx=1`.  
  `i=2`: `abs(a[2])=3.0` not greater.  
- Assign `index = 1`, `max = 10.0`.  
- `chksum = 10.0 + 1.0 = 11.0`.  
Outer loop 2:  
- Reset `max=5.0`, `index=0`, `k=1`.  
- Same → `index=1`, `max=10.0`.  
Return `max + index + 1` = `12.0`.

Same result. So why does the unit test say inconsistent?

float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;

L5:

        chksum = max + (float) index;

}

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // First loop: compute all absolute values and find max
        float max_val = max;
        int max_idx = 0;
        int current_k = k;
        
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[current_k]);
            if (abs_val > max_val) {
                max_val = abs_val;
                max_idx = i;
            }
            current_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Second loop: just update k for consistency with original
        // (This could be optimized away, but kept for semantic equivalence)
        k = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            k += inc;
        }
        k = 0; // Reset for the update below
        
        // Update the original variables
        index = max_idx;
        max = max_val;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
        
        // Restore k to its expected final value
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

Source code: 
```c
float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;
            max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
            k += inc;
        }
        chksum = max + (float) index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // First loop: compute all absolute values
        float abs_vals[LEN_1D];
        abs_vals[0] = max;
        int current_k = k;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            abs_vals[i] = fabsf(a[current_k]);
            current_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Second loop: find max and index (no branching)
        float max_val = abs_vals[0];
        int max_idx = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            int is_greater = abs_vals[i] > max_val;
            max_val = is_greater ? abs_vals[i] : max_val;
            max_idx = is_greater ? i : max_idx;
        }
        
        // Update original variables
        index = max_idx;
        max = max_val;
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:

     ```c
    // Original

        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)

    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;

Source code: 
```c
float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;
            max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
            k += inc;
        }
        chksum = max + (float) index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // Precompute all absolute values into a temporary array
        float* abs_vals = (float*)malloc(LEN_1D * sizeof(float));
        abs_vals[0] = max;
        int current_k = k;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            abs_vals[i] = fabsf(a[current_k]);
            current_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Find max and index without branching
        float max_val = abs_vals[0];
        int max_idx = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            int is_greater = abs_vals[i] > max_val;
            max_val = is_greater ? abs_vals[i] : max_val;
            max_idx = is_greater ? i : max_idx;
        }
        
        // Update original variables
        index = max_idx;
        max = max_val;
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
        
        free(abs_vals);
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

**2. Semantic comparison**  

**Source code logic:**  
- Loop over `i = 1` to `LEN_1D-1`, with `k` starting at `inc` and incrementing by `inc` each iteration.  
- If `fabsf(a[k]) > max`, update `max` and `index = i`.

Wait — `chksum` is overwritten each inner loop, but only `max` and `index` persist to the next outer iteration? Actually, `max` and `index` are reinitialized each outer iteration (`nl` loop), so `chksum` is just a temporary variable.  
- The function returns `max + index + 1` after the last outer iteration.

**Optimized code logic:**  
- First pass: find `local_max` (correct reduction).  
- Second pass: find first index where `abs_val == local_max`.  

**Problem:**  
In the source code, `index` is updated **only when a new strictly greater max is found**, not when equal to current max.  
In the optimized code, the second pass finds the **first index where value equals the final max**, which could be **different** from the source code’s `index` if the max appears earlier but is later surpassed and then equaled again at the end.

- i=3, a[3]=9 ≤ 10 → no update  
Final index = 1.

Second pass: first index where value == 10 is i=1 → index=1 (same here).

- i=1, a[1]=10 > 5 → max=10, index=1

float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;

        index = 0;

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // First pass: find max value (reduction operation)
        float local_max = max;
        int local_k = k;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[local_k]);
            if (abs_val > local_max) {
                local_max = abs_val;
            }
            local_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find first index where value equals max
        // This maintains original semantics (first occurrence, not last)
        local_k = k;
        int first_index = 0;
        int found = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[local_k]);
            // Check if this is the first occurrence of the max value
            if (!found && abs_val == local_max) {
                first_index = i;
                found = 1;
            }
            local_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Update original variables
        index = first_index;
        max = local_max;
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

**2. Semantic comparison with simple test cases**

**Source code behavior:**  
- Finds the **first** index `i` where `fabsf(a[k])` is **strictly greater** than the current `max`.  
- If `fabsf(a[k]) <= max`, it jumps to `L5` without updating `index`.  
- At the end, `index` is the **first** index where a new max was found (strictly greater).  
- If all values are equal to initial `max`, `index` stays 0.  
- If a later value equals `max` but never exceeds it, `index` is not updated.

**Optimized code behavior:**  
- First pass finds the **global max** (correct).  
- Second pass finds the **first index** where `abs_val == local_max`.  
- This changes semantics:  
  Example: `a = [5, 3, 5]`, `inc = 1`.  
  Source:  
    - Start: max = 5, index = 0.  
    - i=1: 3 <= 5 → index stays 0.  
    - i=2: 5 <= 5 → index stays 0.  
    - Final index = 0.  
  Optimized:  
    - First pass: max = 5.  
    - Second pass: first index where value == 5 is i=0? Wait, second pass starts at i=1 (local_k = inc).  
      Actually: `found_index` initialized 0, loop i=1..LEN_1D-1.  
      At i=2, abs_val == max → found_index = 2.  
    - Final index = 2.  
  **Mismatch**.

Let’s test with `a = [5, 3, 5]`, `LEN_1D=3`, `inc=1`, `iterations=2`:

Source:  
Iteration 1:  
- max = 5, index = 0.  
- i=1: 3 <= 5 → no change.  
- i=2: 5 <= 5 → no change.  
- chksum = 5 + 0 = 5.  
Iteration 2: same → final return max + index + 1 = 5 + 0 + 1 = 6.

Optimized:  
Iteration 1:  
- First pass: max = 5.  
- Second pass: i=1 → 3 != 5, i=2 → 5 == 5 → found_index = 2.  
- index = 2, max = 5, chksum = 5 + 2 = 7.  
Iteration 2:  
- First pass: max = 5 (unchanged).  
- Second pass: same found_index = 2.  
- index = 2, max = 5, chksum = 7.  
Final return: 5 + 2 + 1 = 8.  

**Different result** → semantics are wrong.

---

**3. Why the mismatch**  
The source code’s `index` is the **first index where a new max (strictly greater) is found**, not the first index equal to the global max.  
The optimized code’s second pass finds the first index **equal to** the global max after the first element, which is not equivalent.

Also, the source code updates `index` immediately when a **strictly greater** value is found, and that index is the loop counter `i`, not `k`.  
`k` is just the array offset (`k = i * inc` effectively, but starting at 0 then incrementing by inc each iteration).

---

**4. Conclusion**  
Semantics are inconsistent → **FAIL**.

---

**5. Suggestions for fixing**  
The optimized code should keep the original algorithm’s logic:  
- Single pass, tracking `max` and `index` when `fabsf(a[k]) > max`.  
- To vectorize, we must handle the reduction with `max` and also track the first index where max updates, but that’s trickier because `index` update depends on order (first occurrence of greater value).  
- A vectorized approach could find the global max in one pass, then in a second pass find the **first index** where `fabsf(a[k]) == global_max` **and** `i < first_greater_index`? Wait, that’s still not matching original if there are equal values before the greater one. Actually, original only updates index on **strictly greater**, so equal values don’t change index. So second pass should find the first strictly greater transition? That’s not just equality check.

Better: Keep original algorithm but remove the `goto` for vectorization:  
Replace  
```c
if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) goto L5;
index = i;
max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
```
with  
```c
float abs_val = fabsf(a[k]);
if (abs_val > max) {
    index = i;
    max = abs_val;
}
```
This is the same logic, just structured. Then compiler can try to vectorize the max reduction, but `index` is problematic because it’s a dependent variable (needs to know which iteration had the new max first). This is a “max value reduction with argmax” problem, but argmax must be the **first** index where max updates, not any index of max. This is not a standard associative reduction (order matters), so automatic vectorization may fail.

---

**Final analysis:**  
The optimized code changed semantics → **FAIL**.  
Fix requires preserving the strict greater-than update rule for `index`.

Source code: 
```c
float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;
            max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
            k += inc;
        }
        chksum = max + (float) index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // First pass: find max value (reduction operation)
        float local_max = max;
        int local_k = k;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[local_k]);
            if (abs_val > local_max) {
                local_max = abs_val;
            }
            local_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find first index where value equals max
        // This maintains original semantics (first occurrence, not last)
        local_k = k;
        int found_index = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[local_k]);
            // Check if this is the first occurrence of the max value
            if (abs_val == local_max && found_index == 0) {
                found_index = i;
            }
            local_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Update original variables
        index = found_index;
        max = local_max;
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

**2. Semantic comparison**  

The source code does:  
- Iterates through `a[0], a[inc], a[2*inc], …`  
- Keeps track of the **maximum absolute value** seen so far (`max`)  
- Keeps track of the **first index** (in loop iteration `i`) where `fabsf(a[k])` **strictly exceeds** the previous maximum.  
- At the end of each outer loop iteration, `index` is the iteration number (`i`) where the **last new maximum** was found.

Source code: 
```c
float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;
            max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
            k += inc;
        }
        chksum = max + (float) index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // First pass: find global maximum value (reduction operation)
        float local_max = max;
        int local_k = k;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[local_k]);
            if (abs_val > local_max) {
                local_max = abs_val;
            }
            local_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find first index where value is strictly greater than previous max
        // This preserves original semantics
        local_k = k;
        float running_max = max;
        int found_index = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[local_k]);
            if (abs_val > running_max) {
                running_max = abs_val;
                if (found_index == 0) {
                    found_index = i;
                }
            }
            local_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Update original variables
        index = found_index;
        max = local_max;
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Preservation Requirements**:

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

        chksum = max + (float)index;

## **2. Semantic comparison**

The original code:

- Finds **last index** `i` (in loop iteration order) where `fabsf(a[k])` is **strictly greater** than the running max seen so far.
- At the end, `index` is that last `i` where the new max was found (0-based from the start of the scan, not the actual array index `k`).
- `chksum = max + (float)index` is computed but not used later.
- Returns `max + index + 1`.

The optimized code:

- Second pass: finds the **maximum** value among `abs_vals[i]` (including `i=0`? Wait, `local_max` is initialized to `max = fabsf(a[0])`, and loop `i=1..LEN_1D-1` checks `abs_vals[i]`).
- Third pass: finds **last index** `i` where `abs_vals[i] == local_max`.

**Difference**:  
In the original, `index` is the **last iteration `i` where `fabsf(a[k])` was strictly greater than the running max**.  
In the optimized, `last_index` is the **last iteration `i` where `abs_vals[i]` equals the final max**.

These are **not** the same when there are repeated maximum values.

float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;
            max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:

        }
        chksum = max + (float) index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}
```

        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {

        index = 0;

            abs_vals[i] = fabsf(a[current_k]);
            current_k += inc;

        // Second pass: find maximum value and track last index where value increases
        // This preserves the original "last increase" semantics

            last_increase_index = is_greater ? i : last_increase_index;

        // Update original variables
        index = last_increase_index;
        max = running_max;
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
        
        free(abs_vals);
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

{

        index = 0;

L5:

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // First pass: compute absolute values and store in local array
        // This removes the fabsf() call from the reduction loop
        float* abs_vals = (float*)malloc(LEN_1D * sizeof(float));
        int current_k = k;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            abs_vals[i] = fabsf(a[current_k]);
            current_k += inc;
        }
        
        // Second pass: find maximum value and track last index where value increases
        // This preserves the original "last increase" semantics
        float running_max = max;
        int last_increase_index = 0;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            // Use direct comparison without branching for vectorization
            int is_greater = (abs_vals[i] > running_max);
            running_max = is_greater ? abs_vals[i] : running_max;
            last_increase_index = is_greater ? i : last_increase_index;
        }
        
        // Update original variables
        index = last_increase_index;
        max = running_max;
        k = LEN_1D * inc;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
        
        free(abs_vals);
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1

   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

float s318_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // Vector-friendly processing
        float running_max = max;
        int last_increase_index = 0;
        
        // Process in chunks
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            // Read current value before incrementing k
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[k]);
            
            // Branchless comparison
            int is_greater = (abs_val > running_max);
            running_max = is_greater ? abs_val : running_max;
            last_increase_index = is_greater ? i : last_increase_index;
            
            // Increment k after reading
            k += inc;
        }
        
        // Update original variables
        index = last_increase_index;
        max = running_max;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

```c
float s318_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // Split the loop: first iteration handled separately to initialize running values
        int running_index = 0;
        float running_max = max;
        
        // Main vector-friendly loop (no branching)
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[k]);
            
            // Branchless comparison using masking
            int is_greater = (abs_val > running_max);
            running_index = is_greater ? i : running_index;
            running_max = is_greater ? abs_val : running_max;
            
            k += inc;
        }
        
        // Update original variables after loop
        index = running_index;
        max = running_max;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:

     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

float s318_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // Split: handle first iteration separately to initialize

        float running_max = max;

        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[k]);
            
            // Branchless update using masking
            int is_greater = (abs_val > running_max);
            running_index = is_greater ? i : running_index;
            running_max = is_greater ? abs_val : running_max;
            
            k += inc;
        }
        
        // Final assignment
        index = running_index;
        max = running_max;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

But there’s a subtlety: the source code initializes `max = fabsf(a[0])` and `index = 0`.  
If later elements have the same `max` but not greater, `index` stays at 0.  
The optimized code’s branchless logic does the same: `is_greater` is only true when `abs_val > running_max`, so equal values don’t change `running_index`.

Source code: 
```c
float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;
            max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
            k += inc;
        }
        chksum = max + (float) index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // Split: handle first iteration separately to initialize
        int running_index = 0;
        float running_max = max;
        
        // Main loop without branching
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[k]);
            
            // Branchless update using masking
            int is_greater = (abs_val > running_max);
            running_index = is_greater ? i : running_index;
            running_max = is_greater ? abs_val : running_max;
            
            k += inc;
        }
        
        // Final assignment
        index = running_index;
        max = running_max;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

Compiler feedback says:

This is because `running_index` depends on `i` via a condition, and `running_max` is a max reduction, but `running_index` is not a straightforward reduction (it’s an “argmax” pattern).  
The compiler cannot see `running_index` as a reduction because its update is not associative/commutative in a straightforward way for vectorization.  

The original loop has a dependency: each iteration’s `max` affects whether `index` updates — this is a loop-carried dependency for `index` and `max`.  

The optimized code still has that dependency (just written branchlessly).

We could vectorize finding `max` and then find index in a separate pass, but that changes semantics if there are duplicate max values (source picks first occurrence).  

Given the compiler feedback, the current optimized code is **not** auto-vectorizable due to the argmax reduction.

Yes, there is an opportunity:  
We could restructure to compute max in vectorized form, then find the first index matching that max in a second loop. We can store both max and index in a struct reduction (max, index), but that’s not auto-vectorizable in C without compiler-specific reduction clauses (OpenMP SIMD).
