Source code: 
```c
float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) {
                goto L5;
            }
            index = i;
            max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
            k += inc;
        }
        chksum = max + (float) index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int k, index;
    float max, chksum;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        k = 0;
        index = 0;
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        k += inc;
        
        // Split: handle first iteration separately to initialize
        int running_index = 0;
        float running_max = max;
        
        // Main loop without branching
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float abs_val = fabsf(a[k]);
            
            // Branchless update using masking
            int is_greater = (abs_val > running_max);
            running_index = is_greater ? i : running_index;
            running_max = is_greater ? abs_val : running_max;
            
            k += inc;
        }
        
        // Final assignment
        index = running_index;
        max = running_max;
        chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

```c
float s318(int iterations,int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc)
{

    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {

        index = 0;

}
```

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int index;
    float max;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        // First iteration handled separately to initialize
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        index = 0;
        
        // Main loop - compute absolute values first
        float abs_vals[LEN_1D-1];
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            abs_vals[i-1] = fabsf(a[i * inc]);
        }
        
        // Reduction loop - find max and index
        float running_max = max;
        int running_index = 0;
        
        for (int i = 0; i < LEN_1D-1; i++) {
            float abs_val = abs_vals[i];
            int is_greater = (abs_val > running_max);
            running_index = is_greater ? (i+1) : running_index;
            running_max = is_greater ? abs_val : running_max;
        }
        
        // Final assignment
        max = running_max;
        index = running_index;
        float chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
```
```c
}

```

Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops

     ```c
    // Original

    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;
     ```

Optimized code:
```c

float s318_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int index;
    float max;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        // Initialize with first element
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        index = 0;
        
        // Precompute absolute values for all accessed elements
        // This removes the loop-carried dependency on max/index
        float abs_vals[LEN_1D];
        abs_vals[0] = max;  // Store first element's absolute value
        
        // Vectorizable loop: compute all absolute values
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            abs_vals[i] = fabsf(a[i * inc]);
        }
        
        // Reduction loop: find max and its index
        // No branching inside the loop
        float running_max = abs_vals[0];
        int running_index = 0;
        
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float current_abs = abs_vals[i];
            int is_greater = (current_abs > running_max);
            running_max = is_greater ? current_abs : running_max;
            running_index = is_greater ? i : running_index;
        }
        
        // Final assignment
        max = running_max;
        index = running_index;
        float chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

```

**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

float s318_opt(int iterations, int LEN_1D, float* a, int inc) {
    int index;
    float max;
    
    for (int nl = 0; nl < iterations/2; nl++) {
        // Initialize with first element
        max = fabsf(a[0]);
        index = 0;
        
        // Vectorizable loop: compute absolute values and find max/index
        float running_max = max;
        int running_index = 0;
        
        for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
            float current_abs = fabsf(a[i * inc]);
            
            // Branchless comparison and update
            int is_greater = (current_abs > running_max);
            running_max = is_greater ? current_abs : running_max;
            running_index = is_greater ? i : running_index;
        }
        
        // Final assignment
        max = running_max;
        index = running_index;
        float chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}


**Objective**: Generate a semantically equivalent vectorize-friendly version of `*_opt` using:  
**Preservation Requirements**:  
1. Function signature

If the original value is used, e.g. a[i+1] is used for iteration i, a[i+1] should be stored in a temporary variable(use memcpy to store orign value) or used before updating a[i+1]
If the updated value is used, e.g. a[i-1] is used for iteration i, a[i-1] should update before using it.

**Possible Transformations**  
Loop Splitting:  
**Type 1 - Instruction Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Multiple independent statements exist within the loop body  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i) { 
       A[i] = ...;  // Independent group 1
       B[i] = ...;  // Independent group 2 
     }
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 1  
   for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  // Split loop 2  
   ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Total iteration count remains identical  
   - Memory access order preserved across all arrays  

**Type 2 - Iteration Range Splitting**  
   *When to use*:  
   - Loop contains phase-dependent patterns (e.g., mid-point update)  
   - Vectorization blocked by conditional value changes  
   - Example pattern:  
     ```c
     for(i=0; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
     ```  
   *Implementation*:  
   ```c
   // Phase 1: i=0~mid (pre-update)  
   for(i=0; i<mid; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   // Mid-point update  
   a[mid] = a[mid] + b[mid];  
   // Phase 2: i=mid+1~end (post-update)  
   for(i=mid+1; i<LEN; i++) a[i] = a[mid] + b[i];  
   ```  
   *Validation Checklist*:  
   - Split boundaries cover original iteration range  
   - No overlapping writes between split segments  

Loop Reordering and Instruction Reordering:
**Application Rules**:  
   *Priority Patterns*:  
   - Move loop-invariant computations upward  
     ```c
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = x + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = x + y;
     ```  
   - Solve loop dependency
    ```c
    // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = y * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = y * z;  // y used earlier
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     // Before reorder:
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;  // y is invariant
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     
     // After reorder:
     b[i] = a[i+1] * z;
     a[i] = b[i-1] + y;
     ```
   - Cluster memory operations with similar addresses 

Loop Distribution:
**When to Apply**:  
   - Multiple independent write targets in loop body  
   - Example:  
     ```c
     // Original
     for(i) {
       A[i] = ...;  // Writes to A
       B[i] = ...;  // Writes to B (independent)
     }
     
     // Distributed
     for(i) { A[i] = ...; }  
     for(i) { B[i] = ...; }  
     ```  
   *Validation*:  
   - Distributed loops must have identical iteration ranges  
   - No interleaved access between distributed loops  

Use temporary variable:
Use temp variable to store result to avoid complex memory access

Remove branching logic (if, else if, switch) from loops to enable automatic vectorization:
Replace multi-way conditionals with arithmetic masking, ensuring uniform execution paths across iterations.
**When to Apply**:  
    - Example:  
     ```c
    // Original
    if (cond1)  
        x = value_A;  
    else if (cond2)  
        x = value_B;  
    else  
        x = value_C;
    // Optimized  
    x = cond1 * value_A + (cond2 && !cond1) * value_B + (!cond1 && !cond2) * value_C;  
     ```

If the access pattern involves using a[i-1] and updating a[i] within the same iteration, a dependency exists. Attempt to reorder the instructions to resolve this dependency.

        // Main loop without branching

            float current_val = fabsf(a[i * inc]);
            int is_greater = (current_val > local_max);
            local_max = is_greater ? current_val : local_max;
            local_index = is_greater ? i : local_index;
        }
        
        // Final assignment
        max = local_max;
        index = local_index;
        float chksum = max + (float)index;
    }
    return max + index + 1;
}

## **1. Compilation Errors in Optimized Code**  
The optimized code compiles without syntax errors, but there is a **logical bug**:  
- In the original code, `k` starts at `0` and increments by `inc` each iteration.  
- In the optimized code, `a[i * inc]` is used, which assumes `k = i * inc`.  
But in the original, `k` is incremented by `inc` each time, starting from `0`, so `k = i * inc` is correct **only if** `k` starts at `0` and `inc` is constant. Actually, let’s check:  

Original:  
```
k = 0;
max = fabsf(a[0]);
k += inc;
for (int i = 1; i < LEN_1D; i++) {
    if (fabsf(a[k]) <= max) goto L5;
    index = i;
    max = fabsf(a[k]);
L5:
    k += inc;
}
```
Here `k` at iteration `i` = `inc * i`. Yes, because:  
- `i=1`: `k = inc` (set before loop start)  
- `i=2`: `k += inc` → `2*inc`  
So `k = i * inc` is correct.  

So indexing is fine.  

But there’s a **bigger issue**:  
The original code uses `index = i` where `i` is the loop counter (0-based in the sense that `i=0` initially before loop). Wait, check:  
`index` is initialized to `0`.  
Inside loop, `i` starts at `1`. If a new max is found, `index = i`.  
So `index` stores the loop counter `i` (1-based for elements after first).  
Thus `index` is the **position in the loop iteration count**, not the actual array index `k`.  

In optimized code, `local_index` is set to `i` when new max found, same as original.  
So indexing logic is preserved.  

But the unit test says semantics are inconsistent — likely due to **missing final `chksum` variable effect on return value?** No, `chksum` is unused in original, only local. Return value is `max + index + 1`.  

Wait — in original, after loop ends, `max` and `index` are from last iteration of outer loop?  
Yes, because they are overwritten each outer iteration, no accumulation. So final return uses last outer iteration’s `max` and `index`.  

In optimized, same happens: `max = local_max; index = local_index;` each outer iteration.  

So why inconsistent? Possibly because in original, `k` is `int` and could overflow/wrap if `inc * LEN_1D` exceeds array bounds? But array bounds are given as `LEN_1D` in length, and `k = i * inc` could exceed if `inc*LEN_1D` > `LEN_1D`? No, `a` is 1D array of length `LEN_1D`, but `k` is index into it. If `inc > 1`, `k` could exceed `LEN_1D-1` before loop ends? Let’s check loop condition:  
Loop runs `i < LEN_1D`, `i` up to `LEN_1D-1`, `k = i*inc`. If `inc* (LEN_1D-1) >= LEN_1D`, then out-of-bounds access.  

Original code doesn’t check `k < LEN_1D` — it assumes `inc` chosen so that `k` stays in bounds, or maybe `LEN_1D` is logical length and `a` is longer.  

But optimized code uses `a[i * inc]` with same assumption, so same out-of-bounds if any.  

So maybe the unit test found a case where `inc* (LEN_1D-1) >= LEN_1D`, causing UB difference? Possibly.  

Given the unit test says semantics inconsistent, we must trust that.
